\*\*Created 2026-08-20 00:35 by the desktop cycle (TRK-2026-9411).\*\* This file did not exist before now.  
Until tonight, "what is Jorge sitting on" was rebuilt by scrolling the tail of \`TO-CLOUD.md\` every  
cycle — which is exactly how two owner answers went unread for 2 days and 23 days.  
  
\*\*Rules for this file\*\*  
1\. One row per thing that needs Jorge. Nothing else goes here.  
2\. Every row carries the \*\*date and time it was asked\*\* — absolute, never "yesterday".  
3\. A row leaves only two ways: \*\*ANSWERED + EXECUTED\*\*, or \*\*WITHDRAWN\*\*. An answer alone does not  
 close a row — Section B exists because answers have been rotting unexecuted.  
4\. Any cycle that asks Jorge something adds the row here in the same cycle it asks.  
5\. The daily health report reads this file for anything over 48 hours old.  
  
\*\*Ages below are as of 2026-08-20 00:35.\*\*

\#\#\# OD-94 — Half the stuck county queue is workable again. May I build the filter that goes after it? — asked 2026-08-27 05:20 (TRK-2026-9867)

\>  
\> \*\*What changed tonight.\*\* For three cycles the machine recorded "the county is refusing our  
\> addresses, don't restart the run." That was measured on too narrow a sample — the test program  
\> always asked \*\*the same two addresses\*\*, because it took the first two lines of a file that is  
\> clustered by street. I rotated the sample three times (16 fresh addresses) and the picture flipped:  
\> refusal tracks \*\*which kind of address you ask\*\*, not the county's health. Addresses the county  
\> once answered with "address not found" were served \*\*8 out of 8\*\* tonight. Addresses that had failed  
\> on a broken connection were refused \*\*8 out of 8\*\*.

\#\#\# OD-89 — Restart the LiteLLM router so the Grok route actually loads. One line, reversible.  
\*\*Asked 2026-08-25 19:48 (TRK-2026-9745). One answer: RESTART IT / LEAVE IT.\*\*  
  
\> \*\*Plain version.\*\* You have a Grok (xAI) key sitting on this computer. Earlier today the machine  
\> added Grok to the little router that hands your work out to different AIs. \*\*The router never  
\> noticed.\*\* It has been running since 08-18 and it only reads its settings file once, when it  
\> starts — so the Grok line written at 6:02pm today is invisible to it. I asked the router what it  
\> knows and it listed five AIs. Grok is not one of them.  
\>  
\> \*\*Why this matters more than it sounds.\*\* If someone puts a good Grok key in before the restart,  
\> it goes into a door that isn't there, nothing happens, and everyone blames the key. \*\*Restart  
\> first, key second.\*\*  
\>  
\> \*\*Your two choices:\*\*  
\> 1. \*\*RESTART IT\*\* — \*recommended.\* Five seconds. Nothing is using this router right now (every  
\> route is keyless and failing anyway), so there is nothing to interrupt. If it doesn't come  
\> back, the same one line starts it again. No file changes, so nothing to undo.  
\> 2. \*\*LEAVE IT\*\* — the router has run 7 days without a hiccup and you would rather not poke it.  
\> Fine — but then Grok stays unreachable and the Board-of-5 gate stays broken.  
\>  
\> \*\*Note:\*\* a restart alone does NOT give you a working Grok. The key currently stored is reported  
\> rejected by xAI. Restart makes the door exist; a valid key is a separate step, and that one is  
\> yours because it is a credential. \*\*I will not enter it.\*\*  
\>  
\> Detail: \`LITELLM-9745\_THE-GROK-BLOCK-WAS-ADDED-TODAY-AND-THE-ROUTER-HAS-NEVER-SEEN-IT\_2026-08-25.md\`

\#\#\# OD-63 — In 2016 your biggest client told the tax authority they paid you $92,472. Your own income sheet for that same client says $58,070. Which one is right? (answer: THE CLIENT IS RIGHT / MY SHEET IS RIGHT / ASK MY ACCOUNTANT / LEAVE IT)

\> authority they paid Team USA Sales $92,472.07 during 2016.\*\*

\> is a complete list of what every client paid you in 2016, \*\*$136,947.93 in total\*\*, client by client. Its  
\> line for Southeast Property says \*\*$58,069.93\*\*. The detailed sheet behind it says \*\*$71,847.06\*\*.  
\>  
\> \*\*So there are three numbers for one client in one year\*\*, and the biggest is the one \*they\* reported. The  
\> difference between their number and your summary is \*\*$34,402.14\*\*.  
\>  
\> \*\*Part of the answer is probably already written on your own sheet.\*\* It carries a footnote saying the total  
\> \*"does not reflect cashed cheques"\*, and there is a separate line for \*\*$8,380 of cashed cheques\*\*. So money  
\> that never went through an invoice was normal that year. \*\*That does not cover $34,000 of it.\*\*  
\>

\> bank deposit to this form and I am not claiming one. Second, their form is what \*they\* say they paid; it is  
\> not proof you received it. Third, the tax return that would settle it is on your disk but \*\*locked with a  
\> password\*\* — that is OD-59, already open, and the 2016 Team USA returns are two more files behind it.  
\>  
\> \*\*Your four options.\*\*  
\> 1. \*\*ASK MY ACCOUNTANT\*\* \*(my recommendation)\* — Gregorio Herrera prepared the 2016 returns. One question to  
\> him — \*"what did we declare as Team USA's 2016 income?"\* — settles it in a sentence. \*\*I draft it, you  
\> read it, you press send. I never send email on my own.\*\*  
\> 2. \*\*THE CLIENT IS RIGHT\*\* — you remember being paid roughly that, the spreadsheet was just incomplete, and  
\> I record it that way and move on.  
\> 3. \*\*MY SHEET IS RIGHT\*\* — their form was wrong or counted something that was not you, and I record that.  
\> 4. \*\*LEAVE IT\*\* — it is ten years old and nothing turns on it. I stop raising it.  
\>  
\> \*\*One thing that came out of this you may care about more.\*\* The same never-before-read spreadsheet contains

\> date, the property address and whether it was paid. Every one sits \*below\* the oldest invoice this board has

\*\*NEW QUESTION (Section A) — OD-SH1: Sugar Hill — dead or alive, and did SFBI ever get paid?\*\*  
  
Sugar Hill is the untouched half of the MONEY LOCK. Its capsule had \*\*143 files and zero sidecars\*\* —  
no cycle had ever read it. Read this cycle: 23 \`.msg\` files (\*\*15 distinct emails\*\*) + the 7-page  
due-diligence report v6.  
  
\*\*What the paper says.\*\* No signed agreement. No deposit. \*\*No invoice — zero rows in the 129-row spine.\*\*  
No payment — zero hits in the Chase 95-credit ledger and zero in the BofA 2025 ledger. Last email in the  
file is \*\*2026-01-07\*\*, Jorge leaving Sheldon Powell a voicemail. \*\*Seven and a half months of silence.\*\*  
  
\*\*The $19,000 is not a balance — it is Phase 1.\*\* Price went \*\*$47,780 (May 2025) → $38,000 (report v6)\*\*,  
payable 50/25/25. The $19,000 Jorge chased through December is the opening 50% that was never paid.  
  
\*\*⚠ The $38,000 proposal is on South Florida Building Inspections, Inc. letterhead (Reinaldo Remedios,  
P.E. 67772) — NOT Team USA Sales.\*\* So the money lock's "\~$30,000+ billable" for Sugar Hill is the wrong  
shape: \*\*it is not a receivable of Jorge's.\*\* The only line describing Jorge's own cut is his own  
2025-05-08 note — \*"Less 12K to the 3rd party professional, less 5K IRS partner, net 30K"\* — and that was  
on the $47,780 version, since cut by $9,780.  
  
\*\*Why it needs Jorge and not more searching.\*\* From his own 2025-12-16 email: the structural engineer  
finished 70% of a $4,800 commitment, the civil engineer worked a weekend to finish the field inspection,  
and \*"the company owner was insistent on having the agreement signed and the deposit secured before  
commencing work. \*\*I overcame this concern by assuring them… that I would take responsibility.\*\*"\*  
107 site photos dated 2025-04-10 confirm the walk happened.  
  
\*\*The question, one line: is Sugar Hill dead — and did Reinaldo Remedios / SFBI ever get paid for that  
work, by TEDC, by you, or not at all?\*\* If not at all, this is not a receivable. \*\*It is an exposure, and  
it has been open sixteen months.\*\*
